  From Y, no new paths.

- Level 4: From C, I can reach the neighbors I and J.

  - Is node C directly connected to node J? No.

  From I, I can reach the neighbors D and P.
  - Is node I directly connected to node D? Yes.
  - Is node I directly connected to node P? Yes.

At this point, I have found that the shortest path from E to J via the neighbors is E → B → H → C → I → P.

- At D, P, and O, no new connections.

The shortest path from E to J involves traversing 4 edges: E → B → H → I → J.

Other paths with more edges will prove longer. Hence, the path I’ve identified is indeed the shortest.

     - B → N → Y → O → C → I → P → J → D → M → E → F → X → K → G → H

4. **Minimum Cut Set**:
   - Removing the edge (B, H) directly breaks the shortest path.
   - Additional paths involve multiple connections through shared nodes. To break these, we need to cut at strategic points:
     - Removing (N, Y) breaks the path through M and Y.
     - Removing (E, F) ensures that paths through E and F to G are broken.
     - Removing (K, G) ensures that the final connection to H through G is broken.

5. **Verification**:

   - This set of edges is minimal because removing any fewer edges would still leave at least one path between B and H.

Task: task2_min_nodes_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 13
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes to remove in order to completely disconnect the two black nodes (F and J) in the given graph, I will follow these steps:

1. **Identify all paths between the two black nodes, F and J.**
2. **Determine the critical nodes that must be removed to break all paths between F and J.**
3. **Verify that removing these nodes disconnects F and J and that no smaller set of nodes can achieve the same result.**

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify Paths Between F and J:**
   - **Direct Paths:**
     - F -> E -> H -> B -> C -> O -> P -> J
     - F -> E -> H -> B -> N -> O -> P -> J
     - F -> E -> M -> L -> I -> P -> J
     - F -> E -> M -> L -> I -> C -> O -> P -> J
     - F -> E -> M -> L -> I -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J

2. **Determine Critical Nodes:**
   - **Node E:** If E is removed, paths F -> E -> ... -> J are broken.
   - **Node H:** If H is removed, paths through H are broken.
   - **Node I:** If I is removed, paths through I are broken.
   - **Node P:** If P is removed, paths through P are broken.

   However, removing E and P together would already ensure that no path exists between F and J. Let's verify this by considering all possible paths:

   - **Without E:**
     - Paths through E are broken.
   - **Without P:**
     - Paths through P are broken.
   - **Combined Removal (E and P):**
     - Paths through E -> H -> B -> C -> O -> P -> J are broken.
     - Paths through E -> H -> B -> N -> O -> P -> J are broken.
     - Paths through E -> M -> L -> I -> P -> J are broken.
     - Paths through E -> M -> L -> I -> C -> O -> P -> J are broken.
     - Paths through E -> M -> L -> I -> C -> N -> O -> P -> J are broken.

3. **Verify Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing E and P ensures no path exists between F and J.
   - Removing any single node from E or P alone would not suffice, as alternative paths would still exist.

### Final Answer:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [E, P].
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': ['E', 'H']}

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (E and H) in the provided graph, we need to systematically identify all possible paths between these two nodes and ensure that all such paths are severed by removing the minimum number of edges.

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify the Target Nodes:**
   - The target nodes are E and H.

2. ** eksplore Paths from E to H:**
   - We need to list all possible paths from E to H.

   Possible paths from E to H may include:
   - Path 1: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow I \rightarrow H \)
   - Path 2: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \rightarrow F \rightarrow K \rightarrow H \)
   - Path 3: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \rightarrow G \rightarrow K \rightarrow H \)
   - Path 4: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \rightarrow G \rightarrow F \rightarrow K \rightarrow H \)
   - Path 5: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow H \)
   - Path 6: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow K \rightarrow H \)
   - Path 7: \( E \rightarrow P \rightarrow I \rightarrow G \rightarrow K \rightarrow H \)

3. **Identify Critical Edges:**
   - Each path from E to H must be interrupted by removing at least one edge.
   - To minimize the number of edges removed, we need to find the smallest set of edges whose removal will sever all the paths simultaneously.

4. **Evaluate Edge Removal:**
   - Removing edge \( (P, J) \) intercepts paths 2, 3, and 4.
   - Removing edge \( (P, I) \) intercepts path 1.
   - Removing edge \( (C, H) \) intercepts paths 5 and 6.
   - Removing edge \( (G, K) \) intercepts paths 3, 4, and 7.

   Upon closer inspection, we can find that:
   - Removing \( (P, I) \) and \( (G, K) \) intercepts all paths.
     - (P, I) intercepts Path 1.
     - (G, K) intercepts Paths 2, 3, 4, and 7 directly or indirectly via J and K, impacting all related paths.

5. **Final Cut Set:**
   - The minimal cut set that completely disconnects E from H without removing either E or H is:
     - [\((P, I)\), \((G, K)\)]

### Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(P, I), (G, K)]

Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 13
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (B and H) in the given graph, I will follow these steps:

1. Identify all paths between the black nodes B and H.
2. Determine the critical edges that, when removed, will break all paths between B and H.
3. Ensure that the selected edges form a minimal cut set.

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify Paths Between B and H:**

   - Path 1: B-G-H
   - Path 2: B-C-O-I-H
   - Path 3: B-C-O-P-J-I-H
   - Path 4: B-E-G-H
   - Path 5: B-N-Y-O-C-I-H
   - Path 6: B-N-Y-O-P-J-I-H
   - Path 7: B-E-G-X-F-M-J-I-H

2. **Determine Critical Edges:**

   To disconnect B and H, we need to remove edges that are part of all paths between them. From the paths identified, the critical edges are:
   - (B, G)
   - (G, H)
   - (B, C)
   - (C, O)
   - (O, I)
   - (I, H)
   - (B, E)
   - (E, G)
   - (B, N)
   - (N, Y)
   - (Y, O)
   - (O, P)
   - (P, J)
   - (J, I)
   - (E, G)
   - (G, X)
   - (X, F)
   - (F, M)
   - (M, J)

   By removing these edges, we can ensure that all paths between B and H are broken. However, we need to find the minimal cut set.

3. **Minimal Cut Set:**

   Let's consider the minimal cut set:
   - Removing (B, G) and (G, H) will break Paths 1 and 4.
   - Removing (B, C) and (C, O) will break Paths 2 and 5.
   - Removing (O, I) and (I, H) will break Paths 2, 3, 5, and 6.
   - Removing (B, E) and (E, G) will break Path 4.
   - Removing (B, N) and (N, Y) will break Paths 5 and 6.
   - Removing (Y, O) and (O, P) will break Paths 5 and 6.
   - Removing (P, J) and (J, I) will break Paths 3 and 6.
   - Removing (E, G) and (G, X) will break Path 7.
   - Removing (X, F) and (F, M) will break Path 7.
   - Removing (M, J) will break Path 7.

   The minimal cut set that ensures all paths are broken is:
   - (B, G)
   - (G, H)
   - (O, I)
   - (I, H)

   These four edges cover all paths between B and H.

### Final Answer:

     - B → H → E → M
     - B → H → I → J → P → D → J → I → L → M
     - B → H → C → O → P → D → J → I → L → M
     - B → H → C → N → Y → O → P → D → J → I → L → M

   - The path B → H → E → M can be cut by removing (H, E).

   - H is a common node for many paths leading from E.

   - Removing H breaks Path 1 and interrupts several others.

   - Removing P blocks multiple paths converging at P.

   - Removal of just P leaves a path via (E-H-I) intact since, although it doesn't go through P, there are remaining indirect paths.
